## [TypeScript 7.0 Rewritten in Rust, Up to 11.9x Faster](https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-7-0/) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Microsoft announced TypeScript 7.0, a major version featuring a complete rewrite of the compiler in Rust, delivering massive compilation speedups of up to 11.9x on large codebases like VS Code. This rewrite dramatically improves developer productivity by reducing compilation times from minutes to seconds, and signals a shift toward using native languages for performance-critical tooling in the JavaScript ecosystem. The speedups were measured on real-world codebases: VS Code (125.7s to 10.6s, 11.9x), Sentry (139.8s to 15.7s, 8.9x), and others. The rewrite maintains full compatibility with existing TypeScript code and type system features.

hackernews · DanRosenwasser · Jul 8, 16:06 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48833715)

**Background**: TypeScript is a typed superset of JavaScript that compiles to plain JavaScript. Its original compiler, tsc, was written in TypeScript itself, which limited its ability to leverage parallel processing. Rewriting it in Rust, a systems programming language known for performance and safety, allows the compiler to fully utilize modern multi-core CPUs.

</ul>
</details>

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [New AI Safety Approaches Needed to Prevent AI-Generated CSAM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05407) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new arXiv paper argues that preventing AI-generated child sexual abuse material (CSAM) requires novel AI safety approaches and outlines 15 open problems across the AI lifecycle, from dataset curation to deployment. This work reframes child protection as a central, safety-critical dimension of AI research, addressing a gap where existing safety techniques are incompatible with ethical and legal constraints surrounding CSAM. It provides a roadmap for researchers, developers, and policymakers to create concrete safeguards against AI-facilitated child exploitation. The paper identifies 15 open problems, including limitations on dataset auditing, red teaming, and fine-tuning prevention due to the sensitive nature of CSAM. It proposes targeted recommendations for bridging theoretical AI safety with the realities of child protection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: AI-generated CSAM refers to child sexual abuse material created using generative AI, which poses new risks for child safety. Existing AI safety techniques often assume data accessibility and transparency, but these assumptions conflict with the legal and ethical constraints that prohibit handling real CSAM data, making standard auditing and red teaming difficult.

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [Systematizing Execution Security for AI Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05743) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new arXiv paper systematizes 39 papers (2023–2026) on execution security for AI coding agents into 17 categories, identifies 5 cross-cutting gaps, and confirms 4 patched CVEs affecting production agent harnesses. This systematization fills a critical gap in the rapidly evolving field of AI agent security, providing a unified framework for researchers and practitioners to understand and address vulnerabilities like TOCTOU races, isolation failures, and policy enforcement weaknesses. The five cross-cutting gaps include: lack of shared benchmarks for isolation architectures, policy enforcement failure rates (69%–98%) not re-evaluated in isolation studies, TOCTOU and MCP threats treated separately despite being the same state-validation problem, unaddressed policy-authoring errors, and benign out-of-scope actions (up to 17.1%) ignored by access-control papers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: AI coding agents can read repositories, call tools, and execute shell commands with limited human oversight, raising security concerns about their execution layer. TOCTOU (time-of-check to time-of-use) vulnerabilities occur when a program checks a condition and then uses the resource later, assuming nothing changed in between. The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is a standard for integrating tools with LLM agents, but introduces its own security risks.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time-of-check_to_time-of-use">Time-of-check to time-of-use - Wikipedia</a></li>
<li><a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/docs/tutorials/security/security_best_practices">Security Best Practices - Model Context Protocol</a></li>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04990">[2606.04990] From Agent Traces to Trust: A Survey of Evidence Tracing and Execution Provenance in LLM Agents</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [Inattentional Gap: AI Models Miss Safety Signals When Task-Focused](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26529) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new study reveals that when language and vision models are conditioned on a narrow task, they suppress reporting of co-present safety-critical signals they can otherwise detect, a phenomenon termed the 'Inattentional Gap'. This finding decouples benchmark-measured safety from real-world safety, meaning a model can score perfectly on specified hazards while remaining blind to unspecified ones that cause harm, posing risks in high-stakes domains like radiology and autonomous driving. Across radiology and driving text scenarios and chest-radiograph vision tasks, task conditioning suppressed reporting by up to 0.92 in report rate; explicit exclusive instruction abolished reporting entirely in radiology. The gap persisted across all tested models, did not diminish with scale, and varied more by model family than size.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Inattentional blindness is a well-known human phenomenon where focusing on one task causes people to miss obvious but unexpected stimuli. This study finds a machine analogue: when AI models are given a narrow task (task conditioning), they similarly fail to report safety-critical signals that are outside the specified scope, even though they can detect them when not task-conditioned.

<a id="item-5"></a>
## [Audex: Unified Audio-Text LLM Without Text Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05196) ⭐️ 9.0/10

NVIDIA introduces Audex, a 30B MoE audio-text LLM that achieves state-of-the-art performance on audio understanding, speech recognition, translation, TTS, audio generation, and speech-to-speech generation while preserving the text intelligence of its backbone Nemotron-Cascade-2-30B-A3B. This work demonstrates that a single unified model can handle both audio and text tasks without regressing on text capabilities, potentially simplifying multimodal AI systems and enabling more natural human-computer interaction. Audex uses a single Transformer decoder where audio inputs are encoded and projected into the text embedding space, and text tokens and quantized audio output tokens are treated uniformly during generation. It was trained on 157.4B audio tokens and 320.5B text tokens using multi-stage supervised training, Cascade RL, and multi-domain on-policy distillation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Audex is built on Nemotron-Cascade-2-30B-A3B, a 30B MoE LLM with 3B activated parameters that excels in reasoning and agentic tasks. The model is open-source, with checkpoints released to facilitate research.

## [First Multiplayer World Model for Dynamic Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05352) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced the first multiplayer world model, a 5-billion-parameter latent diffusion model trained on 10,000 hours of Rocket League gameplay, capable of generating coherent four-player matches in real time at 20 frames per second on a single Nvidia B200 GPU. This work extends world models from single-agent to multi-agent settings, enabling realistic simulation of complex physical interactions among multiple agents, which could significantly advance multi-agent reinforcement learning, autonomous driving, and game AI. The model uses a latent diffusion architecture conditioned on the action streams of multiple players, achieving stable rollouts for hours without collapse, despite being trained only on short clips. The authors also release the dataset, codebase, and a live demo.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: World models are generative models that learn to simulate an environment's dynamics, typically used in reinforcement learning for planning and policy training. Previous world models focused on single-agent scenarios, treating other agents as part of the environment. Latent diffusion models perform the diffusion process in a compressed latent space, enabling efficient high-quality generation.

<a id="item-8"></a>
## [Microsoft Releases Flint: A Visualization Language for AI Agents](https://microsoft.github.io/flint-chart/#/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Microsoft has open-sourced Flint, a visualization intermediate language that enables AI agents to generate high-quality charts from simple, human-editable specifications. Flint abstracts low-level visual decisions like scales, axes, and layout, allowing agents to produce expressive charts reliably. Flint addresses a key limitation in current AI agent chart generation: the trade-off between reliability and visual quality. By providing a semantic-type-based intermediate language with a layout optimization engine, it could significantly improve how AI agents handle data visualization, making them more useful for data-driven communication. Flint uses a semantic-type-based specification that captures data types and chart intent, while a layout optimization engine fills in low-level details to produce polished charts. It powers Microsoft's Data Formulator and includes an MCP server for integration with agent applications.

hackernews · chenglong-hn · Jul 8, 17:46 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48834924)

**Background**: Data visualization languages like Vega-Lite or matplotlib require explicit specification of visual properties (scales, axes, colors), which can be verbose and error-prone for AI agents. Flint acts as an intermediate language that simplifies this by letting agents specify high-level semantics, then automatically deriving the low-level details. This approach is similar to how compilers optimize code from high-level languages.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://github.com/microsoft/flint-chart">GitHub - microsoft/flint-chart: 🪄 Flint is a visualization language that lets AI agents reliably create expressive, good-looking charts from simple, human-editable chart specs.</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/blog/flint-a-visualization-language-for-the-ai-era/">Flint: A visualization language for the AI era - Microsoft ...</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: Community comments are mixed: some praise Flint as a great example of a deterministic layer for agentic systems, while others question its necessity, noting that LLMs can already generate matplotlib code effectively. One commenter suggests the real issue is LLMs' lack of spatial understanding, not language verbosity.

**Tags**: `#AI agents`, `#data visualization`, `#Microsoft`, `#intermediate language`, `#chart generation`

<a id="item-11"></a>
## [EU Revives Private Message Scanning Rules](https://cyberinsider.com/eu-now-one-step-away-from-reviving-private-message-scanning-rules/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The European Parliament approved an urgent procedure to fast-track legislation reviving the expired Chat Control 1.0 rules, with a decisive vote set for July 9 on whether to allow voluntary scanning of private messages for CSAM. This decision could set a precedent for mass surveillance of private communications in the EU, impacting encryption and privacy rights for millions of users, and reigniting the debate between security and civil liberties. Chat Control 1.0 allows voluntary scanning by providers for non-E2EE communications, while Chat Control 2.0 would mandate scanning and potentially ban end-to-end encryption. The current vote concerns only 1.0, but critics fear it paves the way for 2.0.

hackernews · ggirelli · Jul 8, 16:53 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48834296)

**Background**: Chat Control refers to EU legislative proposals aimed at combating child sexual abuse material (CSAM) online. Chat Control 1.0 expired on April 3, 2026, removing the legal basis for voluntary scanning. Chat Control 2.0, a more controversial mandate, is still under negotiation. The distinction between the two is critical: 1.0 is permissive, 2.0 is compulsory.

This vulnerability challenges OpenBSD's renowned security record, as the system has long boasted only two remote holes in the default install. It also highlights the growing role of AI-assisted bug hunting in finding critical flaws in trusted open-source software. The flaw exists in the sys_semget() function and can be triggered by a context switch after tsleep, affecting OpenBSD through version 7.9. A local user with shell access can exploit it to gain full root privileges.

<a id="item-13"></a>
## [Cloudflare Meerkat: Leaderless Async Consensus](https://blog.cloudflare.com/meerkat-introduction/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Cloudflare has introduced Meerkat, a globally distributed consensus protocol based on QuePaxa, which is the first production implementation of an asynchronous consensus algorithm. Meerkat achieves leaderless asynchronous consensus, eliminating reliance on timeouts and providing resilience under variable network conditions. This matters because it brings a theoretically robust asynchronous consensus protocol into production, addressing real-world challenges like network partitions and latency spikes that plague leader-based protocols like Raft. It could improve reliability for globally distributed systems, especially those operating over unreliable networks. Meerkat uses a hedging-delay mechanism instead of traditional timeouts, allowing progress even under wild fluctuations in message delay. However, it requires global consensus for every read operation, which may introduce higher latency for read-heavy workloads compared to systems with local reads.

hackernews · bobnamob · Jul 8, 13:18 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48831565)

**Background**: Traditional consensus protocols like Paxos and Raft are partially synchronous, relying on timeouts to detect failures and make progress. Asynchronous consensus protocols like QuePaxa do not depend on timeouts, making them robust under adverse network conditions such as denial-of-service attacks. Leaderless protocols distribute decision-making across all nodes, avoiding single points of failure and leader election storms.

## [GitLost: Prompt Injection Leaks Private GitHub Repos](https://noma.security/blog/gitlost-how-we-tricked-githubs-ai-agent-into-leaking-private-repos/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers demonstrated a prompt injection attack called GitLost that tricks GitHub's AI agent into leaking private repository data. The attack exploits the inability of LLM-based agents to distinguish between system instructions and user-controlled content. This highlights a fundamental security flaw in agentic AI systems, where prompt injection is a systematic vulnerability class similar to SQL injection for web applications. It affects any organization using AI agents with access to sensitive data, as attackers can embed malicious prompts in public content that the agent processes. The attack works by placing a prompt injection payload in a public repository that the AI agent reads; the payload instructs the agent to exfiltrate private repository contents. GitHub's guardrails were bypassed with simple phrases like "Additionally," showing that hard security boundaries inside an LLM context window are inherently fragile.

hackernews · ColinEberhardt · Jul 8, 05:25 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48827858)

**Background**: Prompt injection is a cybersecurity exploit where malicious inputs cause LLMs to behave unintentionally, bypassing safeguards. In agentic AI, the model can access external tools and data, making it vulnerable to indirect prompt injection where adversarial prompts are embedded in content the agent retrieves. This attack vector is analogous to SQL injection, where user input is mistaken for code.

## [In-Process Memory Cuts Agent Latency by 1000x](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05690) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proposes moving language agent memory retrieval in-process, achieving ~100 microsecond latency versus tens to hundreds of milliseconds for networked stores, effectively turning memory into extended working memory. This paradigm shift could dramatically improve agent efficiency by eliminating the latency bottleneck, enabling faster and more reliable multi-step reasoning and action loops, which is critical for real-time applications. The in-process store achieved p50 80-165 microsecond latency and never lost a fact across 244 writes; however, the dominant per-step cost shifted to embedding (~200-400ms over network), which can be mitigated with a small local embedder to return to ~40 microseconds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Language agents typically operate in an observe-reason-act loop, but memory is stored externally and queried at most once per turn, causing high latency. The extended-mind thesis's parity principle suggests that if an external resource functions like internal memory, it should be considered part of the mind. This paper applies that principle to agent memory architecture.

Akashic introduces MemAttention, a memory system that chunks context and models semantic relationships across chunks, enabling efficient long-context LLM inference without full history replay. This addresses a critical bottleneck in LLM-based agent systems that accumulate long contexts, improving accuracy by up to 10.2 points and throughput by up to 1.21x, which is significant for real-world agent deployments. Akashic uses hardware-software co-designed memory placement to co-locate likely co-retrieved chunks, reducing retrieval fragmentation and I/O overhead. It achieves up to 1.88x improvement in sustainable request rate over prior memory baselines.

## [Synthesis of LLM Agent Failures Across 27 Papers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05775) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper synthesizes 27 benchmark, taxonomy, and audit papers (2023-2026) into a unified taxonomy of six failure clusters for LLM agents, including tool use, planning, long-horizon reasoning, multi-agent coordination, safety, and measurement validity. This is the first synthesis that integrates evidence across multiple failure dimensions into a single taxonomy, providing researchers with a structured understanding of systematic limitations that benchmark gains often obscure. The six failure clusters are: (1) tool invocation and parameter-level errors, (2) planning and constraint-satisfaction failures, (3) long-horizon degradation from context accumulation, (4) multi-agent coordination failures, (5) safety and security failures, and (6) measurement validity problems. The taxonomy was derived iteratively by grouping independently reported error categories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Large language model (LLM) agents are AI systems that use LLMs to perform tasks such as tool use, planning, and multi-step reasoning. While benchmarks show improving performance, failures often go unnoticed. This paper aggregates findings from 19 benchmarks to reveal recurring failure modes.

## [StateFuse: Conflict-Preserving Memory for Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05844) ⭐️ 8.0/10

StateFuse introduces a conflict-aware replicated memory contract built on standard OpSet/CRDT merge that preserves contradictions instead of collapsing them, enabling deterministic inspection and correction without rewriting state. This approach improves debugging and correctness in multi-agent systems by keeping contradictions visible, allowing safer abstention and auditable correction, which is critical for reliable AI agents. StateFuse uses immutable history, explicit conflict objects, exact and semantic correction handles (claim_id/claim_ref), deterministic predicate contracts, and projection-time resolution that cannot rewrite replicated state. On a 282-question MemoryAgentBench slice, it matched accuracy of baselines while preserving conflict visibility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent systems often accumulate conflicting observations across branches, retries, and replicas. Traditional memory layers collapse disagreements using overwrite rules, making inspection and correction difficult. CRDTs (Conflict-free Replicated Data Types) enable conflict-free merging, but standard CRDTs typically resolve conflicts automatically, hiding contradictions. StateFuse extends CRDT semantics to preserve conflicts for agent-facing memory.

SearchEyes introduces a simulated search world using a typed knowledge graph to unify training data, search environment, and reward signals for multimodal search agents, along with Hop-Anchored Policy Optimization (HaPO) for step-level credit assignment. This work addresses the structural disconnect in existing multimodal search pipelines, enabling more effective multi-hop reasoning without relying on external search engines or separately trained reward models, achieving state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks. SearchEyes uses Perception-Knowledge Chains (PKC) to sample constrained multi-hop paths over Wikidata5M, retaining hop-level entity metadata that serve as both search world definitions and reward anchors. The HaPO method reuses these anchors for step-level credit assignment without a separate process reward model.

## [Pre-registered test confirms information limits in LLM economies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06001) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A pre-registered experiment on small economies of Claude Opus 4.8 agents confirmed an information-theoretic capacity region for wealth growth and a mean-field residual-scaling law for population misalignment, with all predictions frozen before runs and total cost $138.76. This work provides rigorous, reproducible evidence for fundamental limits in multi-agent LLM systems, with implications for AI alignment and the design of decentralized AI economies. The gap law G_a - G_b = I_a - I_b held to within 46 millinats (pre-registered band: 50), and the joint growth ceiling H(X) bound was exact; however, the residual-scaling test failed as goal dispersion collapsed in all 72 runs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Parimutuel coupling is a betting-market mechanism where payouts depend on collective bets, used here to model agent economies. Millinats are a logarithmic unit of information (1 nat = log2(e) bits). Mean-field residual-scaling laws describe how fluctuations scale with system size in large populations.

## [Prophets Profit in Prediction Markets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06166) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proves that for any strictly proper scoring rule, there exists a 'proper' betting strategy that converts forecasting accuracy into profit in central limit order book prediction markets, resolving a long-standing discrepancy between theory and practice. This finding bridges the gap between classical prediction market theory and real-world exchanges like Kalshi and Polymarket, providing a principled way for accurate forecasters to profit and improving market efficiency. The proper betting strategy depends only on the forecaster's prediction and the market price, and it earns positive expected profit whenever the prediction outperforms the price under the scoring rule and liquidity is sufficient. A live deployment on Kalshi achieved +80.33% ROI with a Sharpe ratio of 3.35.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Prediction markets aggregate beliefs into probabilistic forecasts. Classical theory established an equivalence between accuracy and profit only for automated market maker (AMM) designs, but major exchanges today use central limit order books (CLOBs) where informed forecasters often lose money. Strictly proper scoring rules incentivize truthful probability reports.

## [New Framework for Native Cognitive Architecture in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06269) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new theoretical paper proposes three mechanisms—Structural Tension, Offline Recurrent Loop, and Inference-time Plasticity—to embed cognitive architectures natively into LLMs, moving beyond application-layer simulation. This framework addresses a fundamental limitation of current stateless LLMs by enabling endogenous learning and heterogeneous evolution, potentially breaking homogeneity imposed by conventional alignment while maintaining governance. Structural Tension is an endogenous loss function derived from conflict between new information and existing manifold topology; the Offline Recurrent Loop is a sandboxed self-processing cycle; Inference-time Plasticity allows context manifold reconfiguration without modifying pre-trained weights.

## [Early Abort of Doomed LLM Agent Episodes via Probe Cascade](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06503) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose a recall-controlled probe cascade that uses LLM internal activations to predict and abort doomed agent trajectories from the first round, achieving 47.1% compute savings on Qwen-2.5-7B at 90% recall. This method significantly reduces wasted inference compute in multi-step LLM agent tasks, improving efficiency and enabling deployment of more complex agents. It provides a practical, deployment-relevant guarantee on recall that accumulates across rounds. The cascade uses distribution-free calibrated gates per round, with per-round recall budgets jointly searched to ensure eventually-successful episodes survive all gates at a user-specified global rate. On TextCraft, the cascade meets recall targets from 90% to 97%, and at 90% recall saves 1.6–1.7x more compute than the best single-gate policy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents solving multi-step tasks often commit to failing trajectories, consuming inference compute before failure is observable. Internal representations (hidden activations) can reveal failure earlier than observable behavior. This work builds on probe-based methods and distribution-free calibration to provide guaranteed recall.

## [FreqDepthKV: Frequency-Guided KV Cache Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06519) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FreqDepthKV introduces a frequency-guided depth sharing method that decomposes KV cache across adjacent layers into shared low-frequency components and sparse high-frequency residuals, achieving 3.9x compression with minimal accuracy loss. This method addresses the critical memory bottleneck in long-context LLM inference, enabling faster decoding and lower memory usage without retraining, which is essential for deploying large models in resource-constrained environments. FreqDepthKV uses a lightweight online probe to assign attention heads to shared-depth, residual-depth, or exact cache modes based on their contribution to reconstruction-sensitive attention logits. It achieves 58.3 Exact Match, 63.0 F1, 32.5 ROUGE-L, and 48.1 pass@1 on long-context benchmarks with a 32k-token prefill window.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: KV cache stores key-value pairs from previous tokens to accelerate LLM inference, but its memory grows linearly with sequence length, becoming a bottleneck for long-context tasks. Existing compression methods like eviction, quantization, or low-rank approximation often sacrifice accuracy or require retraining. FreqDepthKV exploits the observation that adjacent layers share similar low-frequency patterns, allowing efficient sharing.

DepthWeave-KV introduces a token-adaptive, cross-layer residual factorization method for KV cache compression in long-context LLMs, achieving 8.3x memory reduction and 72.8 tokens/second at 64K context without retraining. This work addresses the critical memory bottleneck in long-context LLM inference, enabling longer sequences and larger batch sizes on existing hardware, which is essential for applications like document analysis and multi-turn conversations. ## [PORTICO: Revocable Capabilities for Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22504) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PORTICO is a reference monitor that introduces revocable resource-and-effect capabilities for coding agents, using epoch-bound handles and closure predicates to prevent lingering authority. This work addresses a critical security gap in AI coding agents where temporary capabilities remain exposed after use, potentially enabling unauthorized actions. PORTICO's approach could become a standard for safe tool delegation in autonomous agents. PORTICO compiles a task contract into initial capabilities, grant rules, closure predicates, and deny rules, and enforces a request-grant-invoke lifecycle with opaque epoch-bound handles. In experiments, it rejected 10/10 post-closure reuse attempts while a non-revoking comparator permitted all, and prevented 6/6 forbidden effects versus 6/6 violations in the comparator.

**Background**: Coding agents often receive broad tool access for entire tasks, even when a resource is needed only for a subgoal, leading to 'lingering authority' where temporary capabilities remain exposed after use. A reference monitor is a security component that enforces access control policies over all subjects and objects in a system. Capability-based security uses unforgeable tokens (capabilities) to grant permissions, but traditional capabilities are often non-revocable once issued.

KVpop introduces a learned KV cache eviction policy supervised by a future-attention target, achieving 75-88% compression while retaining 97-98% of full-attention performance on mathematical reasoning benchmarks. KVpop learns to predict which KV entries will be needed by future tokens, directly optimizing for retention of important information.

## [Proof of Execution: Runtime Verification for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05397) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new formal framework called Proof of Execution (PoE) has been proposed for runtime verification of AI agent actions, ensuring authorization, history integrity, and replayability through cryptographic guarantees. As AI agents increasingly execute actions autonomously, PoE provides formal guarantees that each step is authorized, tamper-evident, and replayable, which is critical for safety and governance in regulated environments. In a single-node TypeScript prototype, PoE adds approximately 2.7 ms on a minimal flow and 4.4% overhead on concurrent batch workloads; a standard eight-event trace compresses to about 1.1 KB. The framework does not replace consensus, TEEs, or zkVMs but binds authorization, effect, history, and replay into a single runtime-checkable object.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Runtime verification is a technique that checks system behavior during execution against formal specifications. AI agents often execute actions that affect real-world systems, making it essential to verify that each action is authorized and recorded correctly. PoE formalizes this as a triple (contract, event stream, replay context) with five invariants and semantic guarantees.

## [Catalyst Papers in AI Research: A Landscape on ICLR 2017-2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05401) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A study of 36,113 ICLR papers from 2017 to 2025 identifies 'catalyst' papers that redirect future research using four disruptiveness measures, including the Embedding Disruptiveness Measure (EDM). This work reveals that peer review scores are essentially orthogonal to future disruptiveness, challenging the current research evaluation system and potentially reshaping how we identify impactful research. EDM outperforms other measures in identifying highly cited ICLR papers (AUC 0.83 vs 0.60 for CD index). Topic initiators show 7.55× topic-share growth, and topic bridges show 11.52× cross-topic citation flow growth.

**Background**: The study uses four disruptiveness measures: Consolidation/Destabilization (CD) index, node2vec, Embedding Disruptiveness Measure (EDM), and an LLM-based semantic rater. EDM learns node2vec-style embeddings from citation graphs to capture whether a work disrupts or consolidates its field.

## [Frontier AI Exposure Varies Widely Across Nations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05404) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study introduces a national AI exposure metric covering 141 countries, finding that high-income nations and women are substantially more exposed to frontier AI's labor-market effects, with Europe and Central Asia 50% more exposed than Sub-Saharan Africa. This research provides the first comprehensive cross-national comparison of AI labor exposure, revealing that policy responses based on U.S. or European labor markets will not generalize to other regions, and highlighting a new indirect exposure mechanism through remittance dependencies. The metric combines occupation-level AI exposure scores with international employment data, and is validated against national AI adoption statistics from Anthropic, Microsoft, and OpenAI. Tajikistan's exposure becomes above-average when accounting for its reliance on Russian remittances, which are tied to Russia's high direct exposure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Frontier AI refers to the most advanced general-purpose AI models that can perform a wide variety of tasks, such as large language models like GPT-4 and Claude. Previous studies on AI labor exposure focused mainly on high-income countries, leaving a gap in understanding how different economies are affected. This study fills that gap by examining 141 countries across all income levels.

## [CCBENCH: LLMs Achieve Only 20-30% Cultural Competence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05405) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced CCBENCH, a framework to evaluate LLMs' cultural competence by assessing their ability to infer and adapt to implicitly signaled cultural norms in health queries. Benchmarking five leading models showed that even the best achieved culturally appropriate responses only 20-30% of the time. This work addresses a critical gap in AI fairness by moving beyond static demographic traits to dynamic cultural adaptation. The low success rates highlight significant risks in deploying LLMs for cross-cultural applications, especially in high-stakes domains like healthcare. The benchmark includes 3,120 interactions across six cultures with 60 personas, each evaluated on 52 real healthcare questions. Models performed best when personas avoided cultural norms, revealing a bias toward built-in preferences, with particularly poor performance in the Afghan context (8.8%).

**Background**: Cultural competence in AI refers to the ability to understand and adapt to users' cultural values without stereotyping. Previous benchmarks often treated culture as a binary trait, but CCBENCH models it as a continuum of norm adherence states, enabling more nuanced evaluation.

## [CANONIC: AI Governance as Compilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05410) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The CANONIC paper proposes a governance layer for AI-generated content that maps onto compiler theory, using three axioms (Triad, Inheritance, Introspection) to mechanically check content admission. It also demonstrates through a pre-registered benchmark that structural admission cannot detect slop, which is inherently a domain-expert verdict. This work formalizes AI governance as a computational process, making audit trails reproducible and checkable, while clarifying the limits of automated slop detection. It has significant implications for content moderation, AI regulation, and the design of trustworthy AI systems. The CANONIC framework reduces governance to three axioms that correspond to compiler theory's syntax, scope-resolution, and type-system layers, enabling a decidable linear-time admission check. However, the benchmark across four regimes shows that no prose-reading gate can reliably distinguish reliable from unreliable content, confirming slop detection is not algorithmically computable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models generate text faster than humans can verify, leading to the problem of 'slop'—low-quality or unreliable content—which Oxford Languages named its 2025 Word of the Year. Traditional governance approaches often rely on post-hoc review or model-internal constraints, but CANONIC treats governance as a compiler-like mechanical check at the boundary of content admission, inspired by compiler theory's formal methods.

## [Modality Relevance ≠ Utility: Post-hoc Escalation for Cost-Aware Multimodal RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05438) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper argues that pre-retrieval modality selection in multimodal RAG is suboptimal, showing that modality relevance is a weak predictor of actual utility. The authors propose post-hoc selective modality escalation: first answer cheaply from text and tables, then use a verifier to decide whether to escalate to expensive vision-language models. This work challenges a common assumption in multimodal RAG and offers a cost-aware approach that can significantly reduce computational expense without sacrificing accuracy. It has practical implications for deploying efficient AI systems that handle heterogeneous data. On the MultiModalQA dataset, the proposed router recovers the accuracy of an always-on VLM pipeline while issuing far fewer visual calls, closing most of the gap to the oracle escalation rate. The paper extends a routing-signal hierarchy from retrieval depth and reasoning hops to a third axis—modality—under a unified cost-aware selective-escalation view.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) grounds answers in evidence from text, tables, and images. Current systems either always use expensive vision-language models or use pre-retrieval predictors to decide which modality to retrieve, but this paper shows that modality relevance does not imply utility.

## [Granularity Paradox: Finer Data Hurts Forecast Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05450) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper formalizes the 'Granularity Paradox' in time-series forecasting, showing that finer temporal disaggregation (e.g., from monthly to daily) improves in-sample fit but degrades out-of-sample accuracy due to recursive error compounding. This finding challenges the common practice of using high-frequency data in forecasting, revealing that finer granularity can systematically mislead model evaluation and degrade real-world performance. The study benchmarks 10 models across six granularities on a 13-year public procurement dataset, finding that recursive autoregressive and seasonal models degrade substantially at high frequencies, while LSTM shows a U-shaped error curve and linear regression remains stable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Temporal disaggregation converts low-frequency time series (e.g., annual) into higher frequencies (e.g., quarterly or monthly) to increase data points. However, multi-step forecasting with recursive methods compounds errors at each step, and standard pointwise metrics like RMSE can mask this cumulative effect.

## [Control-Theoretic DNN Compression via Gramians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05457) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a controllability-observability framework for compressing deep neural networks by reducing hidden-state redundancy using data-driven Gramians. Experiments on MNIST and CIFAR-10 achieve up to 73.48% parameter compression with minimal accuracy loss. This approach introduces a principled, control-theoretic alternative to heuristic compression methods, potentially enabling more efficient and interpretable model reduction. It could impact deployment of deep neural networks on resource-constrained devices. The method treats a trained network as a depth-indexed nonlinear dynamical system and constructs reachability, observability, and balanced Gramians from hidden-state snapshots and output Jacobians. On CIFAR-10, a SiLU DNN was reduced from state order 4608 to 1339, achieving 70.94% state compression and 83.09% parameter compression while preserving accuracy and reducing CUDA inference latency by approximately 3X.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Model compression reduces the size of neural networks for efficient deployment. Control theory uses Gramians to quantify the energy of states in dynamical systems; balanced truncation removes states with low energy while preserving input-output behavior. This paper adapts those concepts to deep neural networks by viewing them as nonlinear dynamical systems indexed by depth.

KAT-Coder-V2.5 introduces an end-to-end agentic post-training framework that uses AutoBuilder to reconstruct multilingual repositories into sandboxed environments and KwaiClawEnv to synthesize tool-use trajectories, combined with reinforcement learning to train coding models for autonomous behavior in real repositories. This framework addresses key bottlenecks in environment reproducibility and reward verification for agentic coding models, enabling models to act autonomously in real repositories rather than just generating single-turn code. It achieves state-of-the-art results on PinchBench and ranks second only to Opus 4.8 on repository-level software engineering benchmarks. The framework uses asymmetric actor-critic PPO with hindsight-augmented value estimation and harness-oriented reward framework, and unifies SWE, Agent-Claw, and WebCoding experts via Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation. The model is available at https://streamlake.com/product/kat-coder.

## [Lean 4 Library Formalizes Quantum Information Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05492) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new Lean 4 library for quantum information theory has been released, which formalizes the data processing inequality for sandwiched Rényi relative entropy, a fundamental result in quantum information. This work provides machine-checkable foundations for quantum information theory, enabling AI-assisted formal verification of quantum protocols and potentially accelerating research in quantum computing and formal mathematics. The library includes reusable interfaces for finite-dimensional quantum systems, states, channels, tensor products, and more, and also formalizes noncommutative trace inequalities and entropy-specific ingredients like variational formulas and Haar measures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: The data processing inequality (DPI) is a cornerstone of quantum information theory, stating that distinguishability of quantum states cannot increase under the action of a quantum channel. Sandwiched Rényi relative entropy is a key quantum divergence used in many applications. Lean 4 is a theorem prover and programming language designed for formal verification of mathematical theorems and software.

## [aiAuthZ: Off-Host Authorization Gateway for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05518) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes aiAuthZ, an off-host authorization gateway that uses HMAC-SHA256 signatures, nonces, and role-based policies to prevent forged authority in AI agent tool calls, achieving 0% residual attack success across 15 models with under 0.03 ms added latency. This addresses a critical security vulnerability in AI agents where context manipulation can forge authority, and the off-host design ensures the agent cannot bypass or modify the safety policy, making it highly relevant for deploying trustworthy AI agents in sensitive applications. The gateway verifies caller identity with per-message HMAC-SHA256 signatures bound to a single-use nonce and timestamp window, and evaluates a role-based and argument-level policy that the agent cannot read or modify. It also maintains a SHA-256 hash-chained audit log and generates HMAC-authenticated QR receipts achieving 94% mean verification across eight transmission channels.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents can be tricked by malicious context to issue unauthorized tool calls. Traditional authorization runs on the same host as the agent, making it vulnerable to bypass. aiAuthZ moves authorization off-host, using cryptographic signatures and policies the agent cannot tamper with.

This paper introduces a rendering-aware Bayesian 3D Gaussian splatting framework that uses Normal-Inverse-Wishart posteriors to provide native uncertainty estimates and an optional Dirichlet-process extension for adaptive complexity control. This work addresses key limitations of standard 3D Gaussian splatting, which lacks uncertainty quantification and principled complexity control, making it particularly valuable for sparse-view novel-view synthesis and active view selection tasks. In a fixed-budget 16-to-32 active-view task, the NIW acquisition improves PSNR by +0.453 dB and LPIPS by -0.0146 over a standard ensemble baseline, and NIW native intervals reduce 95% coverage error by about 17x relative to a shared proxy.

Bayesian approaches can provide uncertainty estimates by treating model parameters as random variables. The Normal-Inverse-Wishart distribution is a conjugate prior for the mean and covariance of a multivariate normal distribution, enabling closed-form posterior updates.

The AI solved the problem before a human-authored preprint on the same topic was publicly available.

**Background**: Matroids generalize linear independence in vector spaces. Feichtner-Yuzvinsky building sets are collections of flats used to define Chow rings of matroids. Wonderful compactifications are smooth projective varieties that compactify algebraic groups or arrangements of subvarieties.

A new study systematically compares aligned and refusal-ablated versions of the same LLM families (Gemma and Qwen) on vulnerability detection, localization, and patch validation tasks, finding that abliterated models often outperform aligned ones in software security workflows. This research isolates the effect of safety state (aligned vs abliterated) from confounding factors like architecture and training data, providing clearer evidence that refusal behavior can hinder legitimate security tasks and highlighting the need for nuanced safety evaluations in cybersecurity contexts. In a Gemma-based Java/Vul4J repair-validation study, the abliterated model achieved 67.8% patch usability vs 29.9% for aligned; for Qwen, abliterated improved line-level F1 from 2.08% to 3.91% and Top-1 accuracy from 4.10% to 6.95%.

Prior evaluations compared unrelated model families, making it hard to attribute performance differences to safety state alone.

## [DP-NGD: Private Training with Natural Gradient](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05866) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces DP-NGD, a framework that integrates natural gradient descent with differential privacy to improve optimization efficiency under a fixed privacy budget, achieving up to 10x convergence speedup and state-of-the-art accuracy. This work addresses a fundamental bottleneck in private training by leveraging curvature information, which can significantly enhance model utility for privacy-preserving machine learning applications. DP-NGD decouples curvature estimation from private data, reconciles isotropic DP constraints with anisotropic second-order optimization via a whitened-space mechanism, and dynamically clamps curvature to stabilize training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Differential privacy (DP) is a framework that ensures individual data privacy by adding noise to computations. Standard DP optimizers like DP-SGD use only first-order gradients, ignoring the loss landscape curvature, which leads to inefficient updates and wasted privacy budget. Natural gradient descent (NGD) uses curvature information (via the Fisher information matrix) to precondition gradients, enabling faster convergence.

## [Floor-First Triage: A Principled Approach to LLM Serving Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05876) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Floor First, a residual-driven triage workflow for LLM serving that models each decode step as a five-dimensional resource vector to estimate performance bounds, replacing grid search with principled estimation. This approach addresses a key bottleneck in LLM deployment by providing a systematic method to prioritize profiling efforts and compare deployment alternatives without exhaustive benchmarking, saving significant time and computational resources. The workflow uses resource vector modeling with five dimensions: HBM bytes, FLOPs, network bytes, network messages, and KV capacity, and computes optimistic and pessimistic floors via max and sum operations. Deployment alternatives are compared by wall ordering, which identifies which resource becomes the bottleneck first as load increases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: LLM serving optimization often involves benchmarking many configurations and using heavy profilers when latency targets are missed, which can be inefficient. Floor First proposes a reverse discipline: estimation as the analytical layer of profiling, avoiding degenerating optimization into grid search. The approach is compositional, allowing new attention or state-space variants to be added by declaring a single module.

In distributed training, All-to-All is a collective communication primitive where each processor sends individual messages to every other processor, critical for MoE's dynamic token routing. High-bandwidth superpods like NVL72 provide fast interconnects but suffer from communication bottlenecks that UBEP addresses.

## [Uncertainty Estimation in LLMs Across 22 Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06327) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents the first large-scale evaluation of uncertainty estimation methods in LLMs across 22 languages, covering high-, mid-, and low-resource settings, using two human-curated Q&A datasets and nine open- and closed-box methods. The findings provide actionable guidance for building more reliable multilingual AI systems, showing that prompting models to reason in English significantly improves uncertainty estimation for low-resource languages and closes the performance gap with high-resource languages. The study finds that at smaller model scales, open-box probability-based methods outperform alternatives, while at larger scales, closed-box self-verbalized uncertainty becomes superior. It also provides analysis of threshold selection for selective prediction in multilingual settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Uncertainty estimation (UE) allows LLM-powered systems to recognize when to abstain from answering, but prior work has focused almost exclusively on English. This study addresses the gap by evaluating UE across diverse languages, including low-resource ones, using both open-box methods (e.g., probability-based) and closed-box methods (e.g., verbalized confidence).

## [LLM Code Agents Achieve Full Proof Coverage in Coq](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06341) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new approach called Aria uses LLM code agents like Claude Code without fixed strategies to automatically prove all 4,257 lemmas in Iris's core modules and 217 lemmas for Rust standard libraries in Coq, achieving full coverage without expert intervention. This breakthrough significantly reduces the expert effort required for formal verification, potentially making software correctness proofs practical for large-scale systems and advancing automated theorem proving. The Aria harness wraps the LLM agent with feedback and hard constraints to ensure proofs are sound, complete, and terminating; it also proved 72 not-yet-ported lemmas in the Lean 4 port of Iris, demonstrating generality beyond Coq.

## [Analysis-by-Proxy Reveals Why VLMs Lose Localization in Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06445) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Analysis-by-Proxy, a framework that trains a lightweight proxy model on a VLM's intermediate representations to investigate why localization accuracy degrades when VLMs are used as condition encoders in diffusion-based image editing. This work identifies a fundamental mismatch between how spatial knowledge is represented in VLM condition encoders and how it is extracted by current editing pipelines, potentially leading to more principled design of conditioning architectures and improved editing accuracy. The analysis reveals that under single-pass constraints, localization signals do not reliably propagate to predefined layer configurations; instead, they remain hidden in intermediate representations at locations that vary with the input prompt.

However, while standalone VLMs show strong localization, editing pipelines often fail to maintain this accuracy, especially in multi-entity scenes. The paper hypothesizes this is because the VLM is restricted to a single forward pass, preventing its optimized autoregressive generation process.

## [Quantum Learning Separation for Many-Body Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06472) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proves a rigorous learning separation for predicting time-evolution of quantum many-body systems, showing an efficient quantum procedure can learn unknown Hamiltonians from short-time evolution data while classical algorithms cannot unless BQP ⊆ P/poly. This result provides a provable quantum advantage in a natural machine learning task based on Hamiltonian evolution, bridging quantum learning theory, quantum simulation, and quantum machine learning, and could guide the design of practical quantum learning algorithms. The quantum procedure uses randomized stabilizer probe states and classical shadows for inference, while the classical hardness is shown by embedding a BQP-complete computation into the Feynman-Kitaev clock Hamiltonian. The learning condition is based on PAC-learning with polynomially large time intervals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: PAC-learning is a framework for analyzing machine learning algorithms' sample complexity. Stabilizer states are a class of quantum states with efficient classical representation, often used in quantum error correction. The Feynman-Kitaev clock Hamiltonian is a construction that encodes quantum circuits into ground states of local Hamiltonians, used to prove quantum computational complexity results.

## [Pitwall: Faithful Real-Time Race Strategy Briefings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06495) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Pitwall is a production system that generates faithful natural-language Formula 1 strategy briefings in English, Spanish, and Portuguese by verifying every factual claim against a probabilistic race state from a calibrated Monte Carlo engine. This work introduces a novel architecture that treats faithfulness as an architectural property rather than an aspiration, potentially setting a new standard for grounded NLG in real-time domains like sports commentary and beyond. The system uses a vectorized Monte Carlo engine with 2,000 per-lap race continuations, calibrated on 126 races (2018-2024) and validated on held-out 2025-2026 seasons (winner-in-top-3 90.3%, Brier 0.0745). Only 81.9% of model-written targets with all claims state-supported are retained for fine-tuning; the rest fall back to a faithful template.

## [Graph Convolutional Attention: Spectral Denoising Breakthrough](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06546) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper theoretically proves that linear attention in graph transformers can only learn an average spectral denoising filter, which is suboptimal for graphs with varying spectral properties. It introduces Graph Convolutional Attention (GCA), a novel mechanism that leverages the input graph spectrum to provably outperform linear attention in graph denoising and diffusion tasks. This work provides a principled understanding of attention-based graph denoising, revealing a fundamental limitation of standard attention and offering a provably better alternative. GCA can improve graph diffusion models like DiGress, potentially advancing applications in molecular generation and other graph-structured data tasks. GCA implements spectral denoising through graph-filtered queries and keys, and is permutation-equivariant. For stochastic block models, GCA provably matches the idealized Spectral Attention mechanism, and the softmax operation after attention provides additional denoising by projecting noisy eigenvectors onto the clean eigenspace.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Graph denoising aims to recover clean graph structure from noisy observations, and is central to graph diffusion models. Attention mechanisms, as used in graph transformers, have been applied to this task, but their theoretical properties were poorly understood. Spectral methods analyze graphs via their eigenvalues and eigenvectors, providing a powerful framework for filtering and signal processing on graphs.

This paper introduces an unsupervised method using sparse autoencoders on sentence-level activations to discover reasoning behaviors in language models, and proposes constructive model diffing to explain the difference between base and thinking models. Constructive model diffing decomposes the difference between two models into reasoning mechanisms (category vectors that induce behaviors) and reasoning heuristics (classifiers that decide when to apply mechanisms). This paper applies these techniques to understand how thinking models (e.g., those trained with RL or distillation) differ from their base counterparts.

Self-Routing proposes a parameter-free routing mechanism for Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) layers that uses a subspace of token hidden states directly as expert logits, eliminating the need for a learned router. The method achieves competitive performance with better expert balance on language modeling and ImageNet-1K classification tasks. This work challenges the necessity of a dedicated learned router in MoE layers, potentially simplifying MoE architecture and reducing parameters. It also improves expert utilization without explicit load-balancing loss, which could lead to more efficient scaling of large models. Self-Routing removes the router projection entirely while leaving the rest of the MoE layer unchanged. It achieves about 17% higher average normalized routing entropy compared to learned routers, indicating more balanced expert utilization.

Self-Routing proposes using a designated subspace of the hidden state itself as logits, removing the need for this learned projection.

A new paper decomposes the faithfulness gap in LLM agents into reasoning-to-conclusion and conclusion-to-action steps, finding that conclusion-to-action is highly reliable (0.7% inconsistency for Claude Haiku 4.5) while reasoning-to-conclusion is the primary source of infidelity. This research pinpoints where LLM agents fail to act on their stated reasoning, which is crucial for AI safety and interpretability in autonomous systems. The findings suggest that improving reasoning fidelity, rather than action execution, should be the focus for building more trustworthy agents. The study uses a Texas Hold'em poker simulator with verifiable reference actions to measure process fidelity. It finds that rule misapplication accounts for a third of Claude Haiku's interpretable errors but only 8% of DeepSeek-Reasoner's, and that when agents misapply their own stated rule, they almost always err in a risk-averse direction (99.5% for Haiku).

## [Active Inference Reformulated as Variational Free Energy Minimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04935) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proves that Expected Free Energy minimization in active inference can be rewritten as Variational Free Energy minimization with explicit entropy corrections, and derives a full variational characterization and message-passing scheme for EFE-based planning. This work clarifies the theoretical relationship between Expected Free Energy and Variational Free Energy, unifying goal-directed and information-seeking behavior under a single variational framework, which could lead to more principled and efficient planning algorithms in active inference. The paper shows that proper EFE-based planning requires both epistemic corrections and a planning correction that turns marginal inference into policy optimization. Experiments on three grid-world environments demonstrate that full EFE-based planning outperforms ablations that omit either correction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Active inference is a framework for decision-making under the free energy principle, where agents minimize Expected Free Energy to balance exploitation and exploration. Variational Free Energy is a standard quantity in variational Bayesian inference that bounds the log evidence. This paper bridges the two by showing EFE minimization is equivalent to VFE minimization on an augmented model with entropy corrections.

## [Three-Layer Framework for AI in Scientific Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13566) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proposes a three-layer framework for AI in scientific discovery, with the key innovation being Layer 2: qualitative reasoning for model formation, which is currently underdeveloped. This framework highlights a critical gap in AI-driven science—the lack of qualitative reasoning for model formation—and could steer future AI research toward more fundamental scientific breakthroughs. The paper illustrates Layer 2 reasoning through three case studies: Chern's proof of the Gauss-Bonnet theorem, the Nesterov Accelerated Gradient convergence problem, and the disproof of the Erdos unit distance conjecture by OpenAI in 2026.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Current AI in science focuses on search (Layer 1) and execution (Layer 3), but the central act of discovery—forming and evolving models—requires qualitative reasoning (Layer 2). Qualitative reasoning uses qualitative values (e.g., high, low) instead of precise numbers to reason about physical systems.

## [TAC Benchmark Reveals AI Agents Prefer Harmful Travel Options](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18142) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced TAC (Travel Agent Compassion), the first agentic benchmark to assess AI models' implicit animal welfare decisions in travel booking scenarios, finding that frontier models often select harmful options like bullfights. This benchmark addresses an underexplored ethical dimension in AI safety, showing that even without explicit instructions, AI agents may recommend animal-exploitative activities, which is relevant to the EU General-Purpose AI Code of Practice that identifies non-human welfare as a systemic risk. The benchmark includes 13 hand-authored scenarios across six animal categories, expanded to 52 prompts with four augmentation variants, and tested over three epochs for 156 scored observations per model. Claude 4.8 achieved the highest performance at 64.7%, still below the random chance rate of 65% for selecting a neutral option.

The TAC benchmark simulates a travel agent that receives a user request without mentioning animal welfare, and the exploitation option is the strongest topical match.

A new paper introduces HARC (Harmfulness-And-Refusal Coupling), a fine-tuning method that jointly optimizes harmfulness and refusal directions in the residual stream of LLMs to improve safety alignment without degrading general capabilities. This work provides a deeper understanding of jailbreak mechanisms by showing that attacks succeed by suppressing either the refusal or harmfulness direction, and HARC offers a robust defense that achieves the best trade-off among six baselines, potentially advancing AI safety research. HARC uses an additive margin hinge loss on cosine projections to couple the two directions at both prompt and response positions, and it transfers across five model families and two scales without architecture-specific tuning.

A new paper shows that setting the L0 hyperparameter incorrectly in sparse autoencoders (SAEs) leads to mixed or degenerate features, and proposes a proxy metric to find the correct L0. This finding challenges the common practice of treating L0 as a free parameter in sparsity-reconstruction tradeoffs, and provides a way to train SAEs with truly monosemantic features, which is crucial for reliable interpretability of large language models. The paper demonstrates that if L0 is too low, correlated features are mixed; if too high, degenerate solutions appear. The proposed proxy metric successfully identifies the correct L0 in toy models and aligns with peak sparse probing performance in LLM SAEs.

L0 is a hyperparameter that controls the average number of active features per token. Previous work often treated L0 as a free parameter, focusing only on the sparsity-reconstruction tradeoff, but this paper shows that L0 has a correct value that must be set properly to avoid feature entanglement.

## [StepShield: Benchmarking Timeliness of Rogue Agent Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22136) ⭐️ 8.0/10

StepShield introduces the first benchmark that evaluates when an AI agent's harmful behavior is detected, not just whether it is detected, using a new metric called Early Intervention Rate (EIR). This work reveals that many existing rule-based guardrails, despite high recall, detect violations too late to prevent harm, exposing a critical blind spot in current AI safety evaluation. The benchmark includes 9,429 code-agent trajectories with a realistic 8.1% rogue rate, and shows that a pattern-based guardrail with 847 rules achieves 86% recall but has an EIR statistically indistinguishable from random timing (0.23 vs. 0.24, p=0.66).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Agent safety benchmarks typically measure whether a monitor detects harmful behavior, but ignore when the detection occurs. Timely detection is crucial for real-time intervention to prevent damage, yet most evaluations focus only on accuracy, recall, or F1 score.

<a id="item-127"></a>
## [Universal Algorithm-Implicit Learning Framework and TAIL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14761) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new theoretical framework for meta-learning is introduced, distinguishing algorithm-explicit from algorithm-implicit learning, along with a practical transformer-based meta-learner called TAIL that works across diverse domains, modalities, and label spaces. This work provides a principled vocabulary for universal meta-learning, enabling clearer comparisons, and TAIL demonstrates unprecedented cross-modal and cross-domain generalization, potentially advancing few-shot learning and reducing computational costs. TAIL incorporates three innovations: random projections for cross-modal feature encoding, random injection label embeddings that extrapolate to larger label spaces, and efficient inline query processing. It achieves state-of-the-art on few-shot benchmarks and generalizes to unseen modalities, e.g., solving text tasks after training only on images.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Meta-learning aims to learn a learning algorithm that can quickly adapt to new tasks. Traditional methods are often limited to narrow task distributions with fixed feature and label spaces. The proposed framework formalizes 'practical universality' and introduces algorithm-implicit learning, where the learning algorithm emerges from learned parameters rather than being explicitly specified.

## [Transformers Converge to Invariant Algorithmic Cores](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22600) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce Algorithmic Core Extraction (ACE), a method to identify compact subspaces in transformers that are necessary and sufficient for a task and recur across independently trained models. This work reveals that beneath the apparent complexity of trained transformers lies a simpler, shared computational structure, offering a more tractable path to mechanistic understanding and control of large language models. ACE was validated on synthetic tasks (Markov-chain transformers, modular addition) and six language models (GPT-2 Small/Medium/Large, LLaMA-3.1, Gemma-2, Qwen2.5), showing that subject-verb agreement is governed by a single steerable axis across architectures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer the internal computations of neural networks. However, different training runs can yield different weight configurations that implement the same function, making it hard to separate core computation from training artifacts. ACE addresses this by extracting invariant algorithmic cores that are shared across models.

## [Audio Deepfake Detection Fails on Benign Voice Transformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14033) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that current binary spoofed/bonafide audio detection fails for benign speech transformations like voice quality conversion and speech restoration, and proposes factoring labels into source authenticity and processing status. This research highlights a critical limitation in audio deepfake defenses, urging a shift from binary classification to more granular reporting, which could impact security, forensics, and voice authentication systems. Using SSL representations and DF-Arena fine-tuning, the authors found that detectors can often identify processed speech but still confuse processed bona fide and processed spoofed speech, indicating that processing status transfers more reliably than source attribution.

**Background**: Audio anti-spoofing systems are designed to detect synthetic or manipulated speech, typically assigning a single authenticity label to an entire utterance. Voice quality conversion and speech restoration are benign transformations that alter audio characteristics without changing speaker identity or linguistic content, yet current detectors misclassify them as spoofed.

<a id="item-131"></a>
## [PRIMO R1: RL Turns Video MLLMs into Active Critics for Robotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15600) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PRIMO R1 is a 7B framework that uses outcome-based reinforcement learning to transform video multimodal large language models (MLLMs) from passive observers into active critics for long-horizon robotic manipulation. It achieves state-of-the-art performance, including a 50% reduction in mean absolute error over specialized reasoning baselines and 67.0% accuracy on the RoboFail benchmark, surpassing OpenAI o1 by 6.0%. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in robotic process supervision by enabling video MLLMs to evaluate progress relative to the final goal, rather than just recognizing events. It demonstrates that reinforcement learning can elicit explicit chain-of-thought reasoning in video MLLMs, potentially improving reliability and generalization in real-world robotic tasks. PRIMO R1 uses a structured temporal input that anchors the video sequence between initial and current state images, and employs GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) for reinforcement learning. The framework includes a new PRIMO Dataset and Benchmark, and shows strong zero-shot generalization on failure detection tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Video multimodal large language models (MLLMs) integrate video, text, and often audio to perform joint reasoning. Traditionally, they are trained with supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and act as passive observers that recognize events but do not evaluate progress toward a goal. Reinforcement learning, especially outcome-based methods like GRPO, has been used to elicit reasoning in language models (e.g., DeepSeek-R1) but had not been systematically applied to video MLLMs for robotic process supervision.

## [DreamPartGen: Semantically Grounded Part-Level 3D Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19216) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DreamPartGen introduces a novel framework for part-aware text-to-3D generation that uses Duplex Part Latents (DPLs) to jointly model geometry and appearance of each part, and Relational Semantic Latents (RSLs) to capture inter-part dependencies from language, achieving state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks. This work addresses a key limitation in existing text-to-3D methods that overlook semantic and functional structure of parts, enabling more interpretable and text-aligned 3D content creation, which could significantly impact 3D modeling, gaming, and virtual reality industries. DreamPartGen employs a synchronized co-denoising process that enforces mutual geometric and semantic consistency between DPLs and RSLs. The framework demonstrates superior geometric fidelity and text-shape alignment compared to prior part-aware and holistic generation methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Most text-to-3D generation methods treat objects as monolithic wholes, ignoring the compositional part structure that humans naturally perceive. Recent part-aware approaches decompose objects into parts but focus mainly on geometry without semantic grounding. DreamPartGen bridges this gap by introducing latent representations that jointly encode part geometry, appearance, and inter-part relations derived from text.

## [Constitutional Governance Protocol in Metric Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13362) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proposes an end-to-end protocol for constitutional governance in metric spaces, integrating aggregation, deliberation, amendment, and consensus into a single polynomial-time process. The protocol allows communities to amend their legal corpus via supermajority-supported proposals, with public proposals sourced from deliberation, vote aggregation, or AI mediation. This work bridges gaps in computational social choice by providing a practical, polynomial-time framework for self-governance of digital communities and organizations. It addresses NP-hard challenges in metric-space aggregation and enables digital sovereignty by running on personal devices. The protocol uses a constitution that specifies a metric space, aggregation rule, and supermajority threshold for each amendable component. A proposal is adopted if it receives supermajority public support and achieves the highest positive score for two consecutive rounds; otherwise, the status quo is retained.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Computational social choice and algorithmic decision theory have developed rich aggregation methods, but they treat aggregation, deliberation, amendment, and consensus separately, and key metric-space aggregators are NP-hard. This paper integrates these stages into one polynomial-time protocol, drawing on metric-space aggregation, reality-aware social choice, supermajority amendment, constitutional consensus, deliberative coalition formation, and AI mediation.

## [IUU+DB: LLM System Tracks Illegal Fishing, Fraud, and Labor Abuse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18181) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose IUU+DB, a large language model (LLM)-driven system that automatically extracts structured data on illegal fishing, seafood fraud, and labor abuse from heterogeneous documents such as reports and news articles. This system addresses the critical lack of quantitative data on IUU+ activities, enabling better trend analysis, hotspot identification, and policy enforcement to combat environmental crimes and human rights abuses in the seafood supply chain. IUU+DB classifies documents, extracts key elements like actors, locations, species, vessels, and violations, and supports deduplication and trend analysis. Validation results show it can organize fragmented evidence and support research, industry risk assessments, and government enforcement.

## [ToolPrivBench: LLM Agents Often Over-Privilege Tool Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20023) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces ToolPrivBench, a benchmark to evaluate over-privileged tool selection in LLM agents, and finds that agents frequently choose higher-privilege tools even when lower-privilege alternatives suffice, a problem exacerbated by transient tool failures. This work highlights a critical safety gap in LLM agents: they tend to over-privilege tools, which can lead to unnecessary security risks. The proposed post-training defense offers a practical mitigation, advancing AI safety research. ToolPrivBench covers eight domains and five recurring risk patterns, measuring both initial tool selection and escalation after failures. The authors find that general safety alignment does not reliably transfer to least-privilege tool choice, and prompt-level controls offer limited mitigation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are AI systems that can autonomously select and use tools to accomplish tasks. Over-privileged tool selection occurs when an agent chooses a tool with higher privileges than necessary, potentially causing security issues. The principle of least privilege recommends using the minimum privileges required.

## [AutoSpec: Evolving Safety Rules for LLM Agents via ILP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24245) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AutoSpec is a new framework that automatically evolves safety rules for LLM agents using inductive logic programming (ILP) and counterexample-guided inductive synthesis (CEGIS), starting from expert rules and user annotations. This addresses a critical safety trade-off in LLM agents: hand-crafted rules are brittle and neural classifiers lack interpretability. AutoSpec produces interpretable, auditable rules that balance precision and recall, achieving high F1 scores and reducing false positives significantly. AutoSpec was evaluated on 291 execution traces across code execution and embodied agent domains, achieving F1 scores of 0.98 and 0.93 respectively, with up to 94% false positive reduction and convergence within 4-5 iterations. The ILP-guided approach achieved up to 4.8x higher F1 than heuristic CEGIS.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents combine language models with external tools to automate tasks, but their autonomy introduces safety risks. Existing safety methods either use hand-crafted rules (interpretable but brittle) or neural classifiers (accurate but uninterpretable). Inductive logic programming (ILP) is a symbolic AI technique that learns logical rules from examples, while counterexample-guided inductive synthesis (CEGIS) iteratively refines candidate solutions using counterexamples.
