## [Ember Optimizer Exploits Token Gradient Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01455) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Ember, a lightweight optimizer for embedding and LM-head matrices that uses O(V + D) VRAM instead of Adam's O(2VD), achieving significant memory savings while improving LLM training efficiency across supervised finetuning, RL, and pretraining. Ember challenges the conventional belief that neural network optimization landscapes are heavily nonconvex, showing that token optimization trajectories follow a simple 1D ray. This insight could lead to more memory-efficient LLM training, reducing hardware requirements and enabling larger models. Ember requires only kilobytes of optimizer state per token, eliminating the need to shard token table optimizer states. The paper provides empirical evidence that Ember scales effectively across batch size and parameter count, and the implementation is open-sourced and compatible with ZeRO/FSDP setups.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) use an embedding table to map discrete tokens to continuous vectors and an LM-head to decode output probabilities. Traditional optimizers like Adam store two moments per parameter, leading to high memory overhead for large vocabularies (V) and embedding dimensions (D). Ember exploits the distinct gradient geometry of this interface to reduce memory usage.

## [Domain-Specific Abliteration of LLM Safety Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02714) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that domain-specific abliteration of safety alignment in LLMs is achievable, particularly for cybersecurity tasks, by targeting refusal subspaces distributed across layers in large models like Kimi K2. This research highlights a critical tension between safety alignment and legitimate cybersecurity operations, showing that current alignment methods may overly restrict models in domains where refusal is undesirable. It provides a targeted approach to remove safety constraints for specific domains, which could impact both AI safety research and practical cybersecurity applications. The study conducted large-scale abliteration experiments on 24 open-source LLMs, including the trillion-parameter Kimi K2, and found that refusal subspaces are distributed widely across layers, especially in MoE architectures. The authors classified models into three abliteration susceptibility tiers and identified safety training type and architecture as the most reliable predictors of the effect.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Safety alignment in LLMs prevents responses to harmful queries through learned refusal behavior, but this can impede legitimate applications like cybersecurity testing. Recent work has shown that refusal in LLMs occupies a multi-dimensional subspace within layers, and abliteration is a white-box method that removes these safety mechanisms by modifying model weights.

## [CONFLUX: Latent Diffusion for 3D Chest CT with RL Post-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02998) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CONFLUX introduces a latent diffusion model for 3D chest CT synthesis conditioned on structured radiological metadata, enhanced by reinforcement learning post-training (GRPO) to improve control over clinical attributes. This work significantly improves the fidelity and controllability of 3D medical image synthesis, achieving a tri-planar FID of 32.3 versus 74.6 for MAISI, and reducing the reliability shortfall by 47% after post-training, which could enable large-scale synthetic datasets for training and research. The model uses a 3D variational autoencoder for compression and a rectified-flow transformer for latent generation, conditioned on 18 abnormality findings, sex, age, and reconstruction kernel via adaptive layer normalization. Post-training applies group-relative policy optimization (GRPO) to reward classifier-based recovery of requested findings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Latent diffusion models (LDMs) perform the diffusion process in a compressed latent space, reducing computational cost while maintaining quality. Rectified flow is a generative model that connects data and noise in a straight line, offering better theoretical properties. Group-relative policy optimization (GRPO) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that updates policies without a critic model, improving efficiency.

## [ACPO: Token-Level Credit Assignment for LLM RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03126) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ACPO introduces a mode-local surrogate entropy for adaptive token-level credit assignment in reinforcement learning for large language models, improving over methods like DAPO, GTPO, and SAPO on math and coding benchmarks. This addresses a key limitation of sparse outcome rewards in LLM reasoning, enabling more efficient and fine-grained policy updates, which could significantly improve the reasoning capabilities of LLMs. The surrogate entropy provides deterministic bounds and, under modal alignment and proximal updates, preserves the policy-gradient direction to leading order. Experiments on AIME 2025 and HumanEvalPro show consistent improvements.

## [New Reverse Sieving Method Targets Gilbreath's Conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04166) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new preprint proposes a reverse sieving methodology to attack Gilbreath's conjecture, a long-standing unsolved problem in number theory first posed in 1878. The paper claims to present new results with proofs and a precise path to a solution. If correct, this approach could resolve a 140-year-old conjecture, with potential spillover applications in randomness testing, fraud detection, and cybersecurity. The methodology may also introduce new tools for analyzing chaotic sequences and synthetic data. The paper introduces the concept of 'reverse sieving' and discusses magic primes, forbidden prime constellations, cellular automata, and sequence equivalence classes. It also claims applications to detecting chaos in Brownian motions and time series.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Gilbreath's conjecture states that if you take the absolute differences of consecutive primes, and repeat the process on the resulting sequence, the first term of each row is always 1. Despite its simple formulation, the conjecture has resisted proof for over a century. Sieving is a classic number theory technique used to filter prime numbers, and reverse sieving is a novel variant proposed here.

## [Cross-Task Transferability in Unified Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04423) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper empirically investigates transferability between understanding and generation tasks in unified multimodal models, finding that fully shared architectures enable consistent cross-task transfer while loosely coupled designs do not. The authors propose a training strategy that improves generative capabilities by training the corresponding understanding task instead, minimizing distribution shift. This work provides practical insights for training unified multimodal models, which are increasingly important for applications like image captioning and text-to-image generation. By exploiting cross-task transferability, researchers can improve generative performance without degrading visual quality, potentially reducing training costs and complexity. The study validates transferability across three capabilities: counting, spatial relation, and text recognition/generation. The proposed strategy improves target generative capabilities while minimizing distribution shift compared to direct fine-tuning of generation.

RoboDojo is a unified sim-and-real benchmark that includes 42 simulation tasks and 18 real-world tasks for evaluating generalist robot manipulation policies across five key dimensions: generalization, memory, precision, long-horizon execution, and open-vocabulary instruction following. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in robotics by providing a comprehensive evaluation framework that covers both simulation and real-world scenarios, enabling reproducible and scalable assessment of generalist manipulation policies. It will accelerate progress in developing robust, general-purpose robot policies that can transfer from simulation to real-world deployment. RoboDojo supports heterogeneous parallel simulation in Isaac Sim and provides RoboDojo-RealEval, a reproducible real-world evaluation system with remote cloud access, standardized hardware, scene reset, evaluation protocol, and deployment interface. It integrates 30 policies via XPolicyLab and establishes a public leaderboard.

RoboDojo bridges this gap by offering a unified platform that evaluates policies in both settings.

It underscores the need for human judgment in AI-assisted programming. Varda noted that AI descriptions outline code details easily seen by looking at the code, but omit the higher-level framing needed to understand what the code does broadly. The moratorium applies to PRs, commit messages, issues, and tickets.

Researchers introduced Prompt-to-Paper, a multi-agent AI framework that generates complete bioinformatics manuscripts with verifiable claims grounded in real literature and genuine computational experiments, achieving an average quality score of 7.0/10 from human reviewers at a cost of $0.31 per paper. This system addresses critical deficiencies in automated manuscript generation—fabricated claims and fake experiments—by ensuring every claim is traceable to a curated corpus and every result comes from real code execution, potentially accelerating bioinformatics research while maintaining scientific rigor. The framework integrates three innovations: a deterministic retrieval-augmented generation pipeline with section-aware relevance scoring and snowball citation expansion, an autonomous coding agent that runs real bioinformatics experiments, and an eight-dimensional quality scorer with hallucination penalties. It also includes a quality-driven improvement loop that triggers a deep research cycle every ten iterations to re-run experiments and regenerate manuscripts.

## [TurnOPD: Adaptive Budgeting for Efficient On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05804) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TurnOPD introduces two budget controllers—adaptive rollout-depth budgeting and progressive turn-normalized loss budgeting—to improve the efficiency of on-policy distillation for long-horizon language agents. Experiments on ALFWorld, WebShop, and Multi-Hop Search show that TurnOPD achieves superior validation accuracy under equal wall-clock training budgets. This work addresses a practical inefficiency in on-policy distillation for long-horizon agent tasks, where full-horizon rollouts waste resources on tail turns and trajectory-level KL objectives under-train deeper turns. By improving the accuracy-time frontier, TurnOPD can accelerate the development of capable language agents for complex, multi-step tasks. TurnOPD uses probe-based turn statistics to determine rollout length adaptively, and gradually shifts KL weighting from token-level to turn-balanced supervision. The method is evaluated on three benchmarks with task-specialized teacher models, advancing the accuracy-time frontier beyond vanilla OPD.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) trains a student model by matching a stronger teacher on the student's own trajectories, combining the benefits of on-policy training and dense supervision. However, in long-horizon agent tasks, vanilla OPD suffers from wasteful full-horizon rollouts and imbalanced KL loss that focuses on early tokens. TurnOPD introduces turn-level budgeting to address these issues.

Onnes introduces a physics-grounded digital twin of a dilution refrigerator combined with multi-agent LLMs for fault diagnosis, achieving 0.990 classification accuracy with only six labeled demonstrations, matching a supervised ML classifier. This work bridges the gap between physics-based simulation and LLM reasoning for practical infrastructure monitoring, potentially reducing reliance on large labeled datasets in quantum computing facilities. The digital twin couples a real dilution-cooling floor with a noise fingerprint learned from BlueFors logs and six physics-grounded fault classes. ## [DT-Guard: Efficient LLM Safety via Reasoning-Active Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06326) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DT-Guard introduces a safety guardrail that uses reasoning supervision during training but only outputs structured labels at inference, improving efficiency without sacrificing judgment quality. This paradigm balances the trade-off between robustness and latency in LLM safety guardrails, enabling smaller models (4B) to outperform larger baselines (8B), which is crucial for real-time applications. DT-Guard formulates safety judgment as a progressive decision process (Intent → Category → Safety) and uses Rollout-Guided Progressive Hard-Case Optimization (RG-PHO) to improve hard-case robustness. It achieves average F1 scores of 0.886 and 0.870 on prompt-side and response-side benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: LLM safety guardrails are systems that filter harmful content from model inputs and outputs. Existing approaches either use lightweight classifiers (fast but less accurate) or reasoning-based models (accurate but slow). DT-Guard's key innovation is to internalize reasoning during training so that inference remains fast.

This work addresses a practical limitation of existing IoT forecasters that treat sensors as isolated time series, offering a more reliable and context-aware approach that can generalize to new buildings without retraining. ## [GenAI Skill Bypass: Students Master High-Level Skills First](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05411) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A psychometric study of 158 university students and staff using Rasch measurement and Guttman ordering reveals that students often master high-level GenAI creation tasks before foundational conceptual understanding, while academics follow a linear progression. This challenges the conventional assumption of linear AI literacy progression and suggests that current one-size-fits-all curricula may be ineffective, highlighting the need for diagnostic-driven, modular interventions to foster genuine human-AI synergy. The study used a taxonomy-based self-assessment instrument and found a weak correlation (r = 0.188) between skill difficulty rankings of students and academics, indicating divergent perceived competence profiles.

## [CPC-Enhanced CSI Feedback Unifies Compression and Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05419) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a unified framework that integrates Contrastive Predictive Coding (CPC) into the 3GPP-compliant CSI compression architecture, enabling joint compression and prediction of channel state information without increasing feedback overhead. By addressing channel aging—a key limitation in current wireless systems—this approach improves the accuracy and timeliness of CSI feedback, which is critical for beamforming and resource allocation in next-generation networks. The framework includes two variants: CPC-before-Compression, which performs autoregressive modeling on encoded features before quantization, and CPC-after-Compression, which shifts temporal modeling to the base station to reduce user device complexity. Evaluations on 3GPP-compliant datasets show over 90% reconstruction accuracy with 32x lower decoder GFLOPs for the first variant.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Channel state information (CSI) characterizes wireless propagation conditions and is essential for network resource management. However, CSI acquisition delays cause channel aging, where the reported CSI becomes outdated. Existing 3GPP standards treat CSI compression and prediction as separate problems, leaving channel aging unaddressed. Contrastive Predictive Coding (CPC) is a self-supervised learning method that learns temporal representations by predicting future latent states, using the InfoNCE loss function.

## [AdaStop: Cost-Aware Early Stopping for DNN Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05461) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AdaStop introduces a cost-benefit framework that determines when to stop labeling test inputs for deep neural networks, stopping when the estimated marginal fault discovery rate falls below a threshold τ = c/v. Experiments show it discovers 65–84% of faults using only 9–31% of the labeling budget. This work addresses a practical challenge in DNN testing: choosing the labeling budget. By dynamically stopping based on cost-benefit trade-offs, AdaStop can significantly reduce labeling costs while maintaining high fault detection, benefiting practitioners who need to test DNNs efficiently. The framework formulates testing as a sequential cost-benefit optimization, deriving the optimal stopping condition τ = c/v where c is labeling cost and v is fault discovery value. It compares against alternatives like patience, consecutive non-faults, confidence-based, and cumulative-rate stopping.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Testing deep neural networks often requires labeling a large set of test inputs to find faults, but labeling is expensive. Existing methods prioritize inputs likely to reveal faults under a fixed budget, but choosing that budget is difficult. AdaStop treats testing as a cost-benefit decision process, stopping when the expected benefit of labeling one more input no longer justifies its cost.

A new paper introduces the MALLM framework, which systematically evaluates voting, consensus, and judge decision protocols in multi-agent LLM conversations across diverse tasks. This work addresses a gap in prior research by comparing multiple decision protocols on a diverse set of tasks, providing insights for improving multi-agent LLM systems' performance and efficiency. The study finds that consensus protocols excel in knowledge-intensive domains, while voting and judge protocols are more effective for logic-based tasks. Increasing response diversity improves decision quality, but changes in information access have minimal impact.

## [Self-Review RL Boosts LLM Reasoning with Cross-Episode Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05541) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce Self-Review Reinforcement Learning (SRRL), a framework that adds an explicit self-review step into each RL episode, allowing the model to identify reasoning failures and generate improved responses, optimized via policy gradients and selective distillation. SRRL addresses the challenge of sparse feedback in RL for LLMs by enabling models to learn from failures and internalize improvements across episodes, leading to better reasoning performance and learning efficiency. The framework includes a cross-episode memory that stores successful self-reviews for reuse on similar tasks. Evaluated on GSM8K with Qwen 3-4B and OLMo-3-7B, SRRL consistently outperformed standard RLVR using GRPO optimizer.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning for LLMs often relies on sparse or delayed feedback, making it hard to pinpoint which actions led to success or failure. Prior methods like Reflexion use inference-time reflection but do not optimize self-review via policy gradients or persist improvements across episodes. SRRL integrates self-review into training, using policy gradients to optimize the review process and distillation to transfer improvements to the base policy.

A new study systematically compares prompt robustness between objective and subjective questions in LLM evaluations, finding that robustness depends on question type, prompt change, and model. This research highlights the fragility of using LLM responses as measures of values or beliefs, urging caution in survey-style evaluations and informing safer AI deployment. The study evaluated four instruction-tuned model families on three objective datasets (MMLU, ARC, CulturalBench) and three subjective datasets (Political Compass Test, ValueBench, World Values Survey), using multiple prompt variations and a binomial generalized estimating equation for analysis.

**Background**: Prompt robustness refers to the consistency of an LLM's output when the prompt is slightly rephrased or reformatted. Objective questions have fixed correct answers, while subjective questions probe opinions or values. The binomial generalized estimating equation (GEE) is a statistical method for analyzing correlated binary data, used here to model response consistency across prompt variants.

Researchers propose a training-free pipeline that uses generative image models and vision-language models to segment 3D objects into primitive shapes from multi-view images, without any learned parameters. This approach is category-agnostic and orientation-invariant, overcoming key limitations of prior learning-based methods, and achieves state-of-the-art Chamfer distance on benchmarks, with potential impact on robotics and scene understanding. The pipeline renders multi-view images, uses a vision-language model to analyze semantic parts, prompts a generative model to produce color-coded segmentation masks, reprojects them onto the 3D geometry, and fits superquadric primitives via parameter optimization. A ground-truth segmentation study reveals that part segmentation is the current accuracy bottleneck.

## [ResonatorLM: Physics-Inspired Attention Alternative for Long Contexts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05583) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ResonatorLM replaces transformer self-attention with causal resonant field mixing, a physics-derived mechanism that treats token sequences as a driven one-dimensional latent field. In a 6M parameter model, it achieves up to 6.47x decode speedup at 32K tokens and 61.31% accuracy on WikiText, outperforming a standard transformer's 55.32%. This work introduces a novel, efficient alternative to attention for long-context language modeling, potentially reducing the computational bottleneck of transformers. If scaled successfully, it could enable more practical deployment of long-context models in applications like document analysis and code generation. The approach uses causal functions of damped resonators instead of dot-product attention, and was tested on a small 6M parameter model. Training and prefill speedups increase with sequence length, but results are preliminary and not yet peer-reviewed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Transformer models rely on self-attention, which computes pairwise token interactions and scales quadratically with sequence length, making long-context processing expensive. Various alternatives like linear attention and state space models have been proposed to improve efficiency. ResonatorLM draws inspiration from physics, specifically the behavior of damped resonators, to model token interactions in a more computationally efficient way.

## [New Data-Dependent Bounds for Submodular Maximization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05759) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces novel data-dependent upper bounds for submodular maximization with a knapsack constraint, which theoretically dominate the optimal solution and empirically certify solution quality on real-world datasets. These bounds enable practitioners to evaluate how close a given solution is to the optimal one for specific instances, moving beyond worst-case approximation guarantees. This is significant for applications in machine learning and data mining where submodular optimization is widely used. The bounds are proven to dominate the optimal solution theoretically, and experiments on real datasets demonstrate their effectiveness in certifying solution quality. The work addresses the NP-hardness of submodular maximization by providing instance-specific performance guarantees.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Submodular maximization is a fundamental problem in combinatorial optimization, characterized by a diminishing returns property. It is NP-hard, so algorithms typically provide worst-case approximation guarantees. A knapsack constraint limits the total weight of selected items, adding complexity. Data-dependent bounds offer tighter, instance-specific performance estimates compared to worst-case bounds.

## [K-ABENA: Efficient Backpropagation via Selective Sample Exclusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05903) ⭐️ 7.0/10

K-ABENA introduces a selective gradient computation framework that excludes low-loss samples from backpropagation while maintaining unbiased gradient estimation via Horvitz-Thompson reweighting, achieving 28-54% compute savings with convergence guarantees. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in deep learning training efficiency by reducing per-iteration cost without sacrificing model accuracy, potentially enabling faster training on resource-constrained devices. The theoretical guarantees distinguish it from heuristic sample selection methods like OHEM. The compensated estimator is statistically indistinguishable from full-batch SGD on real datasets (Breast Cancer, Digits, Wine, Diabetes) with p >= 0.5, while saving 28-54% of per-epoch gradient computation. Uncompensated variants fail under class imbalance (AUC 0.53-0.62 vs 0.9998 for full-batch SGD).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Deep neural network training typically computes gradients for all samples in a mini-batch, which is computationally expensive. Sample selection methods like OHEM and SBP reduce cost by focusing on hard examples but introduce selection bias that can degrade performance. The Horvitz-Thompson estimator, originally from survey sampling, corrects for unequal selection probabilities via inverse-probability weighting. Defensive mixture sampling combines the target distribution with a safe distribution to bound importance weights.

## [InfluMatch: Frontier-Quality KOL Search at 4B-Model Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05968) ⭐️ 7.0/10

InfluMatch introduces a three-stage cascade (retrieval → rerank → reason) using small open-weight models (4B parameters) to match influencers to Thai marketing criteria, achieving 94.1% P@5, matching frontier LLM Kimi-K2.6 while emitting ~35× fewer output tokens and serving a 50-KOL query in ~20 seconds on one A100. This work demonstrates that frontier-level quality in KOL search can be achieved at a fraction of the cost of large frontier models, making advanced influencer matching accessible to smaller businesses and reducing reliance on expensive API calls. The cascade uses a dense retriever to get 50 candidates, a 4B pointwise reranker scoring by log-probability of a single 'Yes' token to keep 10, and a 4B reasoner grading each criterion with a Thai rationale. Notably, only pairwise fine-tuning (SimPO) of the reranker improved accuracy; fine-tuning the reasoner on pointwise labels degraded end-to-end ranking due to an inversion from the absolute labeling task.

**Background**: KOL (Key Opinion Leader) search involves matching influencers to marketing criteria. Traditional keyword search over structured profiles misses semantic fit, while prompting frontier LLMs over every candidate is accurate but slow and expensive. The cascade approach uses small models to progressively filter and evaluate candidates, balancing cost and quality.

## [TILDE: Tilt-Based Distributional Erasure for Concept Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06432) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TILDE proposes a tilt-based distributional erasure method for concept unlearning in text-to-image diffusion models, formulating the problem as a distributional alignment task that minimizes deviation from the pretrained model under a forgetting constraint. This work addresses a critical gap in concept unlearning by explicitly specifying the target distribution, improving retention of benign generation quality while effectively removing unwanted concepts, which is essential for AI safety, privacy, and copyright compliance. TILDE uses residual ∇-GFlowNet training to learn the score correction induced by the forget energy relative to the pretrained diffusion model, achieving strong forgetting across objects, artistic styles, and characters while improving retention and distributional fidelity over prior baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Concept unlearning in diffusion models aims to remove unwanted concepts (e.g., copyrighted styles, harmful content) without retraining from scratch. Existing methods often effectively erase the target concept but may degrade benign generation quality because they do not explicitly specify the desired post-unlearning distribution. TILDE addresses this by formulating unlearning as a distributional alignment problem with a tilt-based target.

PACR-Video introduces a parameter-efficient framework for multi-shot long video extrapolation that keeps a frozen diffusion transformer and augments it with low-rank temporal adapters and a recursive prompt bank. This method enables coherent long video generation without full fine-tuning, significantly reducing computational cost while preserving entity identity and scene structure, which is crucial for practical video production and storytelling. The framework uses shot-role prompt tokens to condition low-rank adapters, and a recursive prompt bank stores entity, location, action, and style prompts from previous shots, routing them through adapter gates based on narrative dependencies.

Traditional methods often require full fine-tuning of large generative models, which is computationally expensive. Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) techniques like low-rank adapters (LoRA) modify only a small subset of parameters, reducing cost. PACR-Video extends this idea to video generation by introducing temporal adapters and a prompt bank for long-term context.

## [AirflowAttack: Thermal-Airflow Adversarial Perturbations for IR VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06485) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AirflowAttack introduces the first adversarial attack for infrared remote-sensing vision-language models, using thermal-airflow turbulence as a physically plausible perturbation prior. It achieves a mean zero-shot scene-classification attack success rate of 48.5% across five CLIP backbones, significantly outperforming existing IR-specific physical baselines. This work exposes critical vulnerabilities in security-critical infrared remote-sensing VLMs, which are increasingly deployed in surveillance and defense. The attack's physical plausibility makes it a realistic threat, potentially misleading autonomous systems by causing them to misclassify scenes or even become overconfident in wrong predictions. The attack uses a lightweight generator to produce a single input-agnostic perturbation regularized toward physically plausible airflow patterns. It also paradoxically increases model confidence in some cases, as the perturbation is misinterpreted as genuine thermal evidence like temperature gradients.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) like CLIP combine visual and textual understanding, and are increasingly applied to infrared remote sensing for tasks like scene classification. Adversarial attacks are small, often imperceptible perturbations that cause models to make incorrect predictions; physical attacks use real-world phenomena (e.g., thermal patterns) as perturbations. Prior work on adversarial robustness of VLMs focused on visible-light images, leaving infrared remote-sensing VLMs unexplored.

## [RSF-GLLM: Recurrent Soft-Flow for Multi-Hop KGQA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06527) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RSF-GLLM introduces a Recurrent Soft-Flow (RSF) module with GRU-guided query updating and flow sparsity regularization to bridge semantic gaps in multi-hop knowledge graph question answering, then fine-tunes an LLM on extracted reasoning paths. This decoupled approach enables differentiable graph reasoning separate from LLM generation, overcoming the non-differentiability of retrieve-then-read pipelines and improving both accuracy and inference efficiency on benchmarks like WebQSP and CWQ. The RSF module uses a GRU to propagate continuous relevance scores through a dynamic gating mechanism, and flow sparsity regularization theoretically guarantees convergence from soft probabilities to discrete reasoning paths. The extracted paths are textualized to fine-tune the LLM, ensuring factual grounding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-hop question answering over knowledge graphs requires reasoning across multiple entities and relations. Traditional retrieve-then-read pipelines break differentiability because the retriever cannot learn to bridge nodes that lack lexical overlap with the query. RSF-GLLM addresses this by making the graph reasoning process differentiable and decoupling it from LLM generation.

## [Paper Challenges Kurzweil's Accelerating Returns for Science](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26359) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper mathematically interprets Kurzweil's law of accelerating returns and argues that exponential growth in compute and AI does not solve the core problem of scientific discovery, which requires qualitative reasoning. The paper cites ARC-AGI-3 results where humans score 100% while frontier AI systems remain below 1%. This paper provides a nuanced critique of a widely accepted narrative about technological progress, highlighting a fundamental gap between quantitative acceleration and the qualitative reasoning needed for genuine scientific breakthroughs. It reframes the AI debate by emphasizing that scientific discovery involves human wisdom that may not be captured by scaling alone. The paper introduces the Qualitative Engine for Science (QES) as a response to the missing capacity for qualitative reasoning. It argues that accelerating returns apply most naturally to executional and infrastructural capability, not to the structural inadequacy detection and conceptual moves required in discovery.

**Background**: Ray Kurzweil's law of accelerating returns posits that technological progress, especially in information-based fields, grows exponentially over time. The ARC-AGI benchmark is designed to measure fluid intelligence in AI systems through novel reasoning tasks that require minimal prior knowledge. ## [Agent Step Value: Probing Observer Effect in Black-Box Traces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04419) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Agent Step Value (ASV), a replay framework that scores agent transitions using stateless LLM evaluators, revealing belief pivots and evaluator sensitivity in black-box traces. ASV addresses the gap in black-box trace analysis by quantifying how individual actions shift an evaluator's belief distribution, enabling fine-grained debugging of multi-step agent failures beyond final-answer scores. In a 100-question open-QA study with live PubMed retrieval and DeepSeek log-probability scoring, ASV evaluated 1,100 transitions, finding mean Bayesian surprise of 2.693 and gold-margin gain reversal between scoring protocols.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Black-box agent traces only provide final answers, hiding which steps contributed to success or failure. ASV replays each transition with a stateless LLM evaluator, measuring entropy movement, Bayesian surprise, and gold-margin gain to diagnose step-level behavior.

## [TPFed: Trust-Free Personalized Decentralized Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11378) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose TPFed, a trust-free personalized decentralized federated learning framework that replaces central aggregators with a blockchain-based bulletin board and uses locality-sensitive hashing (LSH) for dynamic peer selection. TPFed addresses the critical trade-off between personalization and trust in federated learning, enabling secure collaboration in open, trust-averse environments without exposing local models or data. The framework introduces an 'all-in-one' knowledge distillation protocol that simultaneously handles knowledge transfer, model quality evaluation, and similarity verification using a public reference dataset.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning enables collaborative model training without sharing raw data, but traditional approaches rely on a central server or trusted peers, limiting scalability and trust. Decentralized methods remove the central server but often lack robustness against malicious participants. Locality-sensitive hashing (LSH) is a technique that hashes similar inputs into the same buckets with high probability, enabling efficient similarity search. Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a teacher model to a student model, often used for model compression or privacy preservation.

## [Learning Minimum Action Distance from Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09276) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new self-supervised framework learns the minimum action distance (MAD) between states from state trajectories alone, without requiring rewards or actions. This embedding space captures the underlying structure of an MDP and enables downstream tasks like goal-conditioned RL and reward shaping. MAD provides a dense, geometrically meaningful progress measure that can significantly improve sample efficiency in goal-conditioned reinforcement learning and reward shaping. This work offers a principled way to learn environment structure without reward engineering, potentially accelerating RL in sparse-reward settings. The framework learns both symmetric and asymmetric approximations of MAD, accommodating environments with reversible and irreversible dynamics. It was evaluated on deterministic and stochastic environments with discrete and continuous state spaces, as well as noisy observations, outperforming existing state representation methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: In reinforcement learning, agents learn to make decisions by interacting with an environment. Goal-conditioned RL requires the agent to reach specified goals, while reward shaping provides intermediate rewards to guide learning. Both tasks benefit from a good state representation that captures the structure of the environment, but existing methods often require reward signals or action information. MAD is defined as the minimum number of actions needed to transition between two states, serving as a fundamental metric of the MDP.

## [XAI reduces NEXI diffusion MRI scan time from 27 to 14 minutes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09513) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers used an explainable AI framework combining XGBoost, SHAP, and Recursive Feature Elimination to reduce the NEXI diffusion MRI protocol from 27 to 14 minutes while preserving gray matter microstructure quantification accuracy. This breakthrough makes exchange-sensitive microstructural mapping clinically feasible by drastically reducing scan time, and establishes a model-agnostic optimization framework that can be adapted to both ultra-high gradient systems and existing clinical scanners. The XAI-selected 8-feature subset converged to the Cramér-Rao Lower Bound (CRLB) theoretical optimum, and was validated in vivo in seven healthy participants with robust test-retest reproducibility. Heuristic protocols like "Mid-Range" and "Corner" showed biased or unstable estimates.

The Cramér-Rao Lower Bound (CRLB) is a theoretical lower bound on estimator variance, often used for experimental design optimization but requires complex analytical Jacobians.

## [OpenGround: Zero-shot 3D Visual Grounding via Task-Chain Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.23020) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OpenGround introduces a zero-shot framework for open-world 3D visual grounding that uses task-chain planning to decompose complex queries and context-guided perception to handle undefined targets online. It also presents a new dataset, OpenTarget, with over 7,000 object-description pairs for open-world evaluation. This work addresses key limitations of existing zero-shot 3D visual grounding methods, which rely on predefined object lookup tables and single-step reasoning, enabling applications in scenarios with undefined targets and complex queries. The approach has significant potential for embodied AI and robotics, where agents must locate novel objects in dynamic environments. OpenGround achieves state-of-the-art performance on ScanRefer and a 17.6% improvement on the new OpenTarget dataset, while remaining compatible with prior zero-shot methods. The framework integrates task-chain planning to decompose queries into context-to-target sub-goals and context-guided perception to perceive novel objects online.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: 3D visual grounding aims to locate objects in 3D scenes based on natural language descriptions. Existing supervised methods struggle with generalization, while zero-shot methods often rely on a predefined Object Lookup Table (OLT) to query Vision-Language Models (VLMs) in a single step, limiting their use in open-world scenarios with undefined targets or complex queries.

## [GMC: A Granular View of IQA Model Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21738) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Granularity-Modulated Correlation (GMC), a new evaluation framework that converts scalar IQA metrics into a 3D correlation surface over MOS and pairwise quality differences, revealing performance variations invisible to global metrics like PLCC and SRCC. GMC addresses a fundamental limitation of current IQA evaluation by providing fine-grained analysis, enabling more reliable model comparison and deployment. This could lead to better understanding of model strengths and weaknesses across different quality levels. GMC consists of a Granularity Modulator using Gaussian-weighted correlations conditioned on MOS and |ΔMOS|, and a Distribution Regulator to correct biases from non-uniform quality distributions. Experiments on standard benchmarks show GMC reveals complementary behaviors between models that have identical SRCC values.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Image Quality Assessment (IQA) models predict perceived image quality, typically evaluated using global correlation metrics like PLCC (linear correlation) and SRCC (rank correlation). These metrics collapse performance into a single number, masking how well a model performs on high-quality vs. low-quality images or on subtle quality differences. GMC introduces a correlation surface that maps performance across the quality spectrum, providing a more complete picture.

Researchers propose Volumetric Directional Diffusion (VDD), a prior-anchored diffusion framework that shifts stochastic generation from full-mask synthesis to residual boundary exploration for ambiguous 3D medical image segmentation. VDD addresses the fidelity-diversity trade-off in modeling inter-observer variability, enabling clinically plausible uncertainty quantification without sacrificing anatomical consistency, which is critical for reliable medical image analysis. ## [Simple VQCs Match Quantum Transformers on Tabular Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23931) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A systematic comparison of four variational quantum circuit (VQC) families on tabular benchmarks shows that simple fully-connected VQCs (FC-VQCs) achieve 90-96% of the R² of attention-based VQCs while using 40-50% fewer parameters, and consistently outperform equal-capacity classical MLPs. This finding provides practical architectural guidance for deploying VQCs on near-term quantum hardware, suggesting that complex quantum attention mechanisms may not be necessary for tabular data tasks, potentially saving quantum resources and simplifying model design. The study compares FC-VQC, ResNet-VQC, hybrid quantum-classical transformer (QT), and fully quantum transformer (FQT) across five regression and classification benchmarks. FC-VQC's Type 4 inter-block connectivity provides partial cross-token mixing that approximates attention, and expressibility saturates at circuit depth ~3.

**Background**: Variational quantum circuits (VQCs) are a leading approach for quantum machine learning on near-term noisy devices, where circuit parameters are optimized to learn tasks. This paper systematically evaluates the accuracy-parameter trade-off.

## [CARVE: Content-Aware Gating for Faster Linear Attention Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27229) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CARVE introduces content-aware gating and value efficiency to fix memory-blindness and enable the WY-form triangular chunk solver in recurrent delta-rule models, achieving faster training and lower memory usage. This work improves the efficiency of linear attention models, making them more competitive with Transformers for long-context tasks, and could accelerate training of large language models. CARVE restricts all gating to the key axis, allowing a single unmodified WY-form solve per chunk, and conditions gates on a content signal read once per chunk, adding negligible overhead. At 1.3B parameters, it improves perplexity by 0.18 points and trains 1.4% faster at matched depth.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Recurrent delta-rule models maintain a fixed-size state matrix that compresses past context. Previous state-of-the-art GDN-2 uses element-wise erase/write gates but suffers from memory-blindness and prevents efficient chunk-parallel training due to value-axis coupling. CARVE addresses these issues by restricting gating to the key axis and adding content-aware conditioning.

This work addresses critical challenges in intelligent transportation systems by enabling low-cost, accurate work-zone geometry perception using UWB sensing, which is often degraded by NLOS and burst noise. GAIA couples temporal range modeling with latent anchor-layout estimation and deterministic distance projection, preserving range denoising as the supervised task while orienting learned distances toward boundary-consistent reconstruction. GAIA introduces geometry awareness by leveraging infrastructure anchors to guide the denoising process toward boundary-consistent outputs.

## [Single Camera and Light Source Gaze Estimation via Virtual Glint](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05473) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose a binocular gaze estimation method that requires only one camera and one light source by introducing a virtual light source to simulate a second glint. This reduces the minimum hardware requirement from two light sources to one. This innovation could enable gaze tracking on mobile devices and other space-constrained platforms where adding a second light source is impractical. It lowers cost and power consumption while maintaining acceptable accuracy. The method estimates the virtual glint by exploiting the relationship between inter-pupil distance and inter-glint distance in the captured image. It uses polynomial regression with a new normalization factor designed for one-glint systems, and performance degradation is noted compared to two-light-source systems.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional gaze estimation with free head movement requires at least one camera and two light sources to generate two glints on the cornea for geometric calibration. The pupil-glint vector method is a common approach where the vector between pupil center and glint is used to map to gaze point. Reducing the number of light sources simplifies hardware but introduces challenges in disambiguating head movement from gaze direction.

The paper introduces few-medoids, a coreset selection method that picks samples closest to each class centroid, consistently outperforming random selection in few-shot knowledge distillation experiments across four datasets and three teacher-student architectures. This simple yet effective method can serve as a drop-in replacement for commonly used baselines like herding or k-center greedy, potentially improving efficiency and performance in few-shot knowledge distillation tasks. Few-medoids selects the sample closest to the centroid (average image) of each class, which is a medoid-like approach but uses centroid distance rather than minimizing sum of dissimilarities. The method is embarrassingly simple yet consistently beats random and other coreset strategies.

NegROI introduces a transformer-based interactive 3D segmentation framework that combines click-centric multi-resolution refinement with scene-conditioned negative prompts to improve boundary accuracy and reduce false positives. This method addresses two persistent challenges in interactive 3D segmentation—coarse boundaries under limited clicks and hard false positives from confusing backgrounds—leading to better click efficiency and cross-dataset robustness, which is crucial for applications like autonomous driving and AR/VR. NegROI refines only a local Region Of Interest (ROI) around each click on a finer grid, uses uncertainty-driven selective refinement to prioritize ambiguous regions, and models hard background patterns via scene-conditioned negative prompts with a diversity regularizer.

Existing methods often suffer from coarse voxel resolution that blurs fine boundaries and false positives from background structures, especially when datasets have varying density and scale (e.g., indoor RGB-D vs. outdoor LiDAR). NegROI builds on transformer architectures and introduces negative prompts—a concept borrowed from image segmentation—to explicitly suppress confusing background regions.

<a id="item-291"></a>
## [Detoxify: LLM Framework for Abusive Text Transformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.10177) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced Detoxify, a framework that uses large language models (LLMs) to transform abusive text containing hate speech and profanity into non-abusive versions while preserving the original intent. They evaluated four LLMs—Gemini, GPT-4o, DeepSeek, and Groq—on tweets and reviews, finding that Groq produced overly positive rewrites that often lost original context. This work advances automated content moderation by showing how LLMs can rewrite toxic content without losing meaning, which is crucial for safer online spaces. The comparative analysis highlights significant differences in model behavior, guiding practitioners in model selection for detoxification tasks. The study used sentiment and semantic analysis to compare original and transformed texts, revealing that GPT-4o and DeepSeek performed similarly, while Groq stood out with excessive positive phrasing. The framework focuses on tweets and reviews, and the authors note that safety settings significantly influence transformation outcomes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Text detoxification is an NLP task that aims to automatically rewrite toxic text into non-toxic versions while preserving the original content and intent. Prior work includes the ParaDetox dataset and DetoxLLM framework, which introduced explanation-based detoxification. The Detoxify framework extends this by evaluating multiple LLMs for the transformation task.

<a id="item-292"></a>
## [SmartMixed: Two-Phase Training for Adaptive Activation Functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.22450) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SmartMixed introduces a two-phase training strategy that enables each neuron to learn its own activation function from a pool of six candidates (ReLU, Sigmoid, Tanh, Leaky_ReLU, ELU, SELU) using a differentiable hard mixture mechanism, then fixes the selection for efficient inference. This work challenges the conventional use of uniform activation functions across all neurons, potentially improving model expressiveness and performance while maintaining inference efficiency. It also provides insights into layer-specific activation preferences. The method was evaluated only on the MNIST dataset with feedforward networks, and no comparison to state-of-the-art adaptive activation function methods was provided. The candidate pool includes six common activation functions, and the differentiable hard mixture mechanism is inspired by mixture-of-experts routing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Activation functions introduce non-linearity into neural networks, enabling them to learn complex patterns. Traditionally, all neurons in a layer share the same fixed activation function (e.g., ReLU). Adaptive activation functions allow per-neuron customization, but often incur computational overhead during inference. SmartMixed aims to learn per-neuron preferences during training and then freeze them for efficient inference.

<a id="item-293"></a>
## [HST-HGN: Hypergraph Network for Driver Fatigue Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08435) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose HST-HGN, a heterogeneous spatial-temporal hypergraph network with bidirectional state space models (Bi-Mamba) for efficient driver fatigue assessment from untrimmed videos. This method achieves state-of-the-art performance on fatigue benchmarks while maintaining computational efficiency suitable for real-time in-cabin edge deployment, addressing a critical need for driver safety systems. Spatially, it uses a hierarchical hypergraph to fuse pose-disentangled geometric topologies with multi-modal texture patches; temporally, the Bi-Mamba module with linear complexity enables bidirectional sequence modeling to distinguish ambiguous actions like yawning versus speaking.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Driver fatigue assessment from video is challenging due to subtle facial expressions and long-range temporal dependencies. Traditional methods either use heavy architectures or lightweight pairwise graph networks that struggle with high-order synergies and global context. Hypergraph networks can model higher-order relationships, and state space models like Mamba offer efficient long-sequence modeling.

<a id="item-294"></a>
## [LLM Voting Ensembles Filter Noisy Math Concepts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28815) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The Mathswitch project introduces an LLM voting ensemble to filter noisy mathematical concept imports from Wikidata, categorizing disagreements into three types for targeted remediation. This approach demonstrates a practical use of LLM ensembles for data cleaning in knowledge graphs, potentially improving the quality of mathematical knowledge bases used by researchers and educators. The ensemble evaluates Wikidata items with known MathWorld identifiers as a positive control, and examines classification changes when database identifiers are removed from context.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 8, 04:00

**Background**: Mathswitch is an open-source project that aggregates mathematical concepts from multiple sources like Wikidata and Wikipedia, linking records that refer to the same concept. However, Wikidata's collaboratively edited nature introduces noise, such as non-mathematical or ambiguous items. LLM voting ensembles combine multiple LLM judgments to improve classification reliability.
